Nobody gets out alive anyway.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To come out of a situation; to escape a fate."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"He's been in prison for six months, but he gets out next week.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To be released, especially from hospital or prison."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I think copper prices will plummet this fall, so I'm getting out while I can.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To remove one's money from an investment; to end an investment."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"We must get the children out first.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To help (someone) leave."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Get the playing cards out and we'll have a game of snap.","type":"example"},{"text":"It's time to get the Christmas decorations out.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To take (something) from its container or storage place, so as to use or display it."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I'll get out at the end of the road and walk from there.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To leave the inside of a vehicle such as a car. (Note: for public transport, get off is more common.)"],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Somehow the secret got out.","type":"example"},{"text":"We have to act before that fact gets out on us.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To become known."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"You work too hard. You should get out more.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To spend free time out of the house."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The organization has just gotten their newsletter out.","type":"example"},{"text":"This candidate is struggling to get her message out.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To publish or make available; to disseminate."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"He could hardly get the words out for the tears.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To say with difficulty."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"This detergent will get most household stains out.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To remove or eliminate (dirt or stains)."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"School gets out in the afternoon.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To end."],"tags":["intransitive"]}],"sounds":[],"synonyms":[{"sense":"leave or escape","word":"exit"},{"sense":"leave or escape","word":"go out"},{"sense":"help someone leave","word":"remove"},{"sense":"take something from its container","word":"take out"},{"sense":"take something from its container","word":"extract"},{"sense":"become known","word":"transpire"},{"sense":"clean, eliminate dirt or stains","word":"remove"}],"upstream_line":273524,"upstream_offset":1366337213,"upstream_record_sha256":"604d43725c80b12abcbf550429566e18ae0b240f09af7ee41e2d4d86daede1f6","word":"get out"}
